- vlog 极简生活｜回归内心 顺其自然｜从“无为而治”的智慧思考极简生活🧘‍♂️: Kind: captions Language: zh 我想说的是 我们所有持有的物品 都是存在于我们需求之后的 而并不是被商家的宣传 心理种草 无中生有创造出来的一种需求 因为那是我们自己的一种想象 想象当我们持有这件物品之后 生活会变得更好 嘿嘿 简单生活 我又出来了 星期天 天气越来越热 是不是要到夏天了呀 果然是星期天 公园里人好多 今天碎碎念 是因为想起来了 前阵子 和一个前辈经营者去吃饭的时候 聊天聊起来 我在说业务上的管理 人事上的管理… 具体的一些工作上的一些困扰 如果可以的话 想要听听这位前辈的意见 或者是请教一番 结果呢这位前辈说：放っとおけば、うまくいくよ 就是说：“放着别管它就行了”这么个意思 然后我愣了一会儿 我说诶怎么能放着不管呢 后来我琢磨了很久 他应该是说“无为而治”吧 就是说避免过度的管理 适得其反 老子估计也是想说 不能做违反自然的事情和行为 哎跟这些人打交道费脑子 天天看他们高手过招 神仙打架的 说个话都听不明白 后来我也仔细品味了一下 好像是那么回事 在一个创业团队呢 或者是一个事业的初创期呢 我觉得也没有必要过度的去管理 管理的特别细致 特别的具体 当基本制度完善了以后呢 就应该做一些能够给成员 或者员工赋能的事情 而不是去做一些条条框框 去限制他们的发挥 当然也包括我自己在内 我想了想 如果是能够自我学习 自我成长的人呢 一定也能跟着团队一起进步成长 而那些节奏慢或者是自甘堕落的人呢 也会慢慢的落伍掉队 然后也跟不上团队的进步和发展 最终也会脱离团队的核心 不必为了去等待谁 而拖慢整体的节奏和速度 我觉得尤其是在管理这件事情上 所以起初困扰我的管理制度的设计 这么一个难题呢 也就迎刃而解了 那么 这和我们的极简生活又有什么关系呢 以前我特别执着摄影和数码产品 买了这家的相机 又想着那家的镜头什么的 研究各种各样的镜头转接环 买了这个又卖了那个 总是折腾来折腾去的 我想在我们的消费生活里 买一件东西是再方便不过的事情了 进到店里边 付钱拿货走人 或者直接在网店一键下单 次日到货 只要付钱 我们都能把想要买的东西买到家 但是当我们不需要它们的时候呢 想要处理起来 就是一件非常麻烦的事情了 如果是单价低 便宜货 用了很久消耗的差不多的时候 想要把它们扔掉的时候也其实都很OK 也都无所谓 也不会心疼 在日本大件的物品想要扔掉的话 还需要一些垃圾处理费用 如果我想卖掉的话 我会拿去二手商店把它处理掉 以一个非常低廉的价格 当然 因为这些二手回收商店回收之后 也不确定什么时候能卖得出去嘛 如果是挂在メルカリ 或者是闲鱼这样的网上去卖呢 也不知道需要挂上多久才能卖得出去 在卖出去之前呢 依旧还是占用家里的空间 依旧还是惦念着这件事情 送人？送人就别提了吧 送给谁好呢 总之物品入手的时候非常的简单 非常的容易 想要出手的时候 却变成了一件非常困难 特别复杂的事情 关键是还要影响心情 费一番周折 所以作为极简主义者的我呢 在购买高单价或者是大件物品的时候 就特别的慎重 在买之前 我就开始思考 当我不需要它的时候 应该怎么样去处理它更合适 其实用老子的“无为而治” 顺其自然 放任不管 来思考这件事情的话 也是挺合适的 并不是说我们什么都不管 什么都不做 爱咋咋地 而是 并不必花太多的时间和精力去思考 管理这些 纠结这个纠结那个 甚至成了一种精神内耗 “哎呀我要是这个机身配那个镜头 是不是重量不平衡啊” “我要是那个高解析力的镜头 配这个低像素的机身 是不是有点浪费” 最后，一张照片还没有拍呢 搞得我很累 比如 我不必思考我的EDC里边需要有什么 当我时常需要一支笔的时候 我的EDC里边自然就会出现一支笔 而并不是被商家的宣传 心里种草 然后觉得自己想要这个想要那个 然后我们就乖乖的去消费 乖乖的就买回家 所有的广告宣传 基本也都是一个意思嘛 “您值得拥有” 以前我还是一个人单身生活的时候 并不持有洗衣机 是因为我居住的公寓附近 就有投币洗衣房 而并非家里似乎要有一个洗衣机 就非要去买一个洗衣机不可 对于“无为而治” 放任不管 顺其自然的思考 是回归我们的内心 关注我们自身真正的需求 需求产生了 那件商品 那个物品就自然会出现 而并非看到了某件商品某个物品 需求就被他们“勾引”出来了 做一个简单的总结：“无为而治”（口误） 这些思考 对于我们的极简生活也有很大的帮助 每做一个抉择 每付出一种行动 都是对于我们的时间精力金钱 甚至是情绪的一种消耗和消磨 与其是被外部的推广宣传信息轰炸 不如回归到我们自己的内心 关注我们自身真正的需求！ 好了 这是今天的内容 我是linlinxing 在东京简单生活碎碎念 如果你有什么想法 欢迎留言评论

- 从穷人思维到富人思维 Change your mindset to wealth: Kind: captions Language: zh-Hant 今天過得好嗎？ 今天再來聊聊金錢 是擁有100萬好呢 還是擁有能賺100萬的認知好呢 顯然是擁有能賺100萬的認知比較好 因為只有你認知到了 擁有多少財富就是一個順其自然的結果 相反 如果一個人沒有與100萬匹配的認知 就算是今天給他100萬 相信一段時間之後 他會再次失去這個100萬 因為憑運氣賺來的錢 一定會靠實力還回去 當認知不匹配的時候 會有千千萬萬種方法來收割你 財富僅僅靠守是守不住的 一個人能擁有和掌握多少的財富 靠的是成熟穩定的心性 匹配的認知 願不願意投資 提高自己的認知是一個人從窮人思維到富人思維 轉變的關鍵點 分享三個觀點 第一點，事業的規則是價值交換 我們每個人自身掌握的資源 能力都是非常有限的 所以要學會從別人身上 獲得信息和資源 運用別人的力量來成長自己 發展自己 這才是最高效的成長路徑 社會運行的規則之一就是你有價值 我有價值 在誠信平等的基礎之上 相互的付出價值交換 讓彼此的合作實現一加一大於二的共贏 通過價值交換讓別人增值 讓自己更有價值 當你自身的價值越來越大的時候 別人才會主動的靠近你 別人也會更加樂意的 給你提供其他的價值 你就不會缺信息缺資源 任何單方面付出的關係都不能持續 第二點，財富不是目標，是手段 現在很多人把財富當做人生的目標 真相是追著錢跑的人都不會賺到大錢 錢不應該是最後的目標 而是你做成了某件事情 創造了多少的價值 對這份價值的一個外在的體現的形式 人生真正重要的不是你擁有多少錢 而是你想活成什麼樣子 錢是幫你實現從你現在這樣到你想的那樣的一個工具而已 比如說我的人生目標之一 是希望做一個通透的智者 這樣的智者 就可以給很多處於困惑和迷茫的人一些價值 這些價值就可以兌換成財富 所以在通往智者的路上 我願意把自己當下的時間 精力金錢投入到學習 連接優秀的人 體驗生活當中去 讓這些資源切切實實的發揮作用 提高我的內在價值 第三點，多買資產，理性消費 20幾歲上班的時候 看著身邊圈子裡面的很多人 都在討論買了什麼品牌的包 什麼品牌的表 我也很羨慕 但考慮現狀一直沒買 我把僅有的資金去旅行 去讀書學習 去發展興趣愛好 2013年買了一輛小車車 方便自己去周邊城市 參加一些商業活動 找不懂的行業實習體驗的機會 當時那些錢估計也能夠買幾個香奈兒的入門款 但放到現在 估計我也不會再背 大概也是要當做廢品處理掉 多年過去 也真正的明白人真正的底氣 不是你背了什麼包 帶了什麼表 而是來自內在的力量 改性的穩定 認知的提升 理性的消費 不要被資本裹協 不要在物質層面沉淪 懂得為自己想要的價值而付費 以上是對財富價值認知的分享 希望看視頻的寶寶都能活出自己的高價值 擁有自己想要的人生狀態 堂堂正正立足於社會之中 獲得自己該擁有的財富人生

- 我在美國講儒家：無神論p.k.有神論？ 中國人沒信仰？不。中國人自古有精神高度【今日儒家】Teaching Confucianism in America: Kind: captions Language: zh-Hant 我自己呢，在美國的時候 有很多次地向美國的百姓 介紹儒學的這樣壹個的場合、壹些機會 其中我註意到 有幾個點是特別打動他們的 我在介紹儒家的時候，我會講 我說 但是我們會講，我說： 這樣的壹個精神高度，是根植於人的內心 它是壹種內在的超越 它不是壹種外在的這種超越 這壹點，當時昊坤感覺到 很多人聽到之後 誒，好像眼睛壹亮 這些很多人當中，有些人是 他曾經有過信仰的這種經歷 但是他感覺他還在不斷地尋找 人生的這種 更符合他自己感受的這樣的壹種狀態 另外壹個點就是 我們儒家，孔子壹直強調的 我們兩千五百年的傳統，就是 「子不語怪力亂神」 就是孔子不講這些 神秘的、禍亂的、怪異的，這些的內容 我記得當時有壹次我在談 或者說介紹儒家的時候 底下有壹位聽眾就跟我說 說：妳講這個東西太重要了 我們有很多從事科學方面研究的 這樣的壹些研究者 他們當下就很需要這樣的壹個信仰 對呀 我記得當時，在波士頓講學，在Lexington 講到這個「子不語怪力亂神」 或者說講到這個「形而上的高度不談神秘」 底下有壹位朋友是區教會的朋友 他應該也是教徒 他說，我們教會…… 人群構成肯定跟別的地方不太壹樣了 更多是知識分子或者是 所謂社會精英嘛 大家坐在壹起開討論會的時候就說 說：這教會再這麽搞，我們也沒法信了 但是他願意信，他不是不想信的意思 他是真誠地願意信仰 但是，神創論也是個坎兒啊 這樣的壹些東西 它跟科學明顯相反的東西 他覺得，我確實過不去心裏這個坎 這時候妳把「不語怪力亂神」給他 他覺得：妳這太好了，妳到我們教會講講這個去吧 我當時就是這個，能不能講到壹塊去還不壹定呢 但是，確實有這樣的壹種困惑 實際上大量的人背離教會，在20世紀的時候 也是從這壹點出發 對，這是壹個很真實的，大家的這種反思 文虛學友呢？ 總地來說，(美國人對儒家) 應該是不了解 就是 大體來說是非常地不了解、非常地陌生 即便是到神學院裏面 因為像芝大、哈佛的兩個神學院是 很主張說："我們是壹個包容的" 是開這個風氣之先，包容多元的宗教 也希望能夠有儒家的聲音進來 但是目前來說，恐怕都沒有真正能夠代表 在我們兩位去之前，恐怕也沒有真正 我們也不敢不敢 就是，還是有壹些不壹樣的 說到儒家還是能夠想到有人在 但是在此之前可能沒有真正的 對於儒家的深入地這樣的壹個了解 但是其實我們在上壹些課上的時候 尤其是在關註到現實問題 解決，比如說他們很關註的生態問題 可持續發展、環境 人類這個種族等等 其實，基於對儒家很淺薄的壹點認識 都希望能夠聽到儒家的這個聲音 其實之前是在，零幾年吧 可能有這個，關於世界生態發展這種大會上 是耶魯的幾個研究中國歷史的學者 然後，就作為壹個儒家的代表 還寫過這樣的類似於宣言 就是來介紹儒家講天人什麽和諧呀 這樣的壹些理論提出來 然後作為那樣的壹個補充 能夠感覺到 他們特別呼喚壹種 人文主義、人本主義的這樣的壹種信仰 來和其他的宗教壹起 彌補其他宗教解決不了的壹些問題 給現實的這些問題提出壹個 更加符合人心、更加 能夠內心完全接受的，這樣的壹個答案 所以感覺到，壹方面是很不了解 但是又好像特別想要去了解壹些儒家的東西 或者說想要把儒家的東西拿到整個的 這樣的壹個平臺當中來 就是在我之前學習東亞研究的這個感覺當中 感覺到，人們對於儒家的這個認識 很多時候，妳真要談到“儒家”的話 從歷史上可能很多是 也是負面的認識比較多 因為就感覺和中國的 “封建”的歷史，是綁在壹起 然後包括，如果我們對中國有壹些不好的 這種認識的話，不好的印象的話 很多時候又會聯想到 是不是妳們 就跟儒家有相關的地方？ 所以其實人們對於儒家的認識 和對於中國歷史、對中國的這種誤解 都是綁定在壹起的 這恐怕是壹個 很普遍的壹個現象 也是 很難壹兩句話說清楚的壹個問題 我更多地還是從學術的角度 關註的還是美國的儒家學術研究 美國社會對於中國文化整體的認知 對於儒家的整體的認知 壹個是 可以說偏於 再有壹個呢 比較陳舊，就是指 20世紀初的這壹些中國學者 當時對儒家的認識 沈澱到了美國的學術界 不是學術界，公共話語權裏 就是由學術界往下沈澱 就到了這個 老百姓的日用層面了 比如說，到美國去 到壹個中文學校，給這些中國小孩講課 問中國小孩：妳們課上教孔子嗎？ “教，講孔子，我們老師跟我們說了孔子” 妳們怎麽講孔子的呀？ 講完說：講了孔子，所以中國人服從權威 “所以中國人，壹直不敢反抗” “亞洲文化就比較地帶有這種懦弱” “而且男權思想重” “對女權不保護” 壹說就是這個 包括教科書也有問題 中國的孩子，我是說華裔的孩子們 看的也是這些書 他自己也就不知道自己的文化 真正有什麽值得驕傲的地方 根本也不關註 這是很多華人父母的壹個心聲 “我想教我孩子中國文化、教我孩子儒家” 那麽，我們把話將心比心 我們如果作為非華裔的美國人 在看待這個群體的話 我們…… 所以說 自己的壹個感覺 很憋屈 很憋屈。 妳華人不往外說，外國人為什麽敬重妳的文化？ 妳自己說的都不到位，他們為什麽能夠...... 妳為什麽埋怨人家不理解？ 而且呢 這點就有點具有現實性了 與我們所想象的不太相同的是 好像我們理解，隨著中國經濟發展的崛起 中國的這個，在國際地位中的重要 西方對中國的研究應該越來越感興趣才對 實際上 不太是那麽回事 這裏面有很深刻復雜的壹個原因 但是我們就傳遞壹個信息： 不是隨著國力的增強，不是隨著經濟的發展 人們就敬重妳的文化 文化的發展自有其線索，自有其思路 妳到底體沒體現出妳這裏面真正深刻的東西 形而上的高度，有高度的東西 才決定人家是不是真正欣賞妳 我們壹般想到這種文化傳播 就把它跟這個宣傳掛鉤 比如說我們怎麽宣傳中國文化好 但實際呢，就是說 深入人心的這種感知 其實往往是民間的這種對話 或者說，我們說人與人之間的這種對話 我自己在學校讀書的時候 我當時就接觸到了我們學校的這個牧師 他是我們學校裏的牧師 當時跟他第壹次見面的時候，我就說 我說：我是壹個儒者 那牧師聽了之後都，既興奮又激動，跟我說： “我們學校每年接待好多中國留學生啊！” 說：我以為他們當中，總得有…… 剛開始(他)是以為大多數人，或者說大家都 都是儒者，或者說都信仰儒家 結果這麽多年 沒有發現中國人有信仰儒家 就是說，我們想從中國的留學生 或者說中國人上，了解儒家 我都沒找到渠道 這次終於來壹個說“自己是儒者” 自己信仰儒家這麽樣壹個(人) 他很激動 然後他當時就邀請我跟他談 然後他就說，他很在乎的是什麽呢？ 他要聽我自己 精神信仰歷程的故事 這個牧師在學校裏 也辦這種同學之間的交流 大家之間互相分享這方面的東西 我就談我自己學習儒學、做儒家修身工夫 這些最打動我的、我自己怎麽樣提升的 尤其是當時我自己怎麽樣 感覺到有過不去的坎、我有我的這種弱點 有內心呢，翻不去的…… 揮之不去的陰影，我是怎麽通過學儒學的跨越的 他當時聽的時候就是壹臉非常欣賞的表情 當時他就認定他說：妳這個儒家談得好 妳在我們學校辦個儒學社吧 面向所有的這些人 包括這些學生也好，社會上的人也好 妳來講這個儒學 我沒有想到，我到美國去之後 是在壹個基督徒的壹個牧師的幫助下 開設了壹個以儒家信仰為核心的 這樣的壹個社團 這剛剛開始這壹點就讓我感受到就是說 他們作為壹些有信仰的人 他認同妳的這個高度 那後來我自己就沿著這樣的壹個方向 開辦下去 進行了跟更多人更深入地對話 在這個過程當中，我自己發現 就是對於 我們壹開始可能學習儒學 儒家的經典啊、歷史啊 特別打動我們 但是我跟美國人講 講什麽這個 東漢的儒者呀、宋明的儒者呀 他反應不過來 他也反應不過來 他連中國歷史朝代表他都不知道 他什麽都不知道 但是，同樣很吸引人的是什麽呢？ 就是，我們作為壹個儒者 怎麽通過修行儒家的工夫 來解決現實當中問題的 這個東西我自己發現，他 不談歷史，不談經典，也有這個打動人的 它超越文化的壹種界限 對，超越文化的壹個界限 有壹次這個交流，我自己就談 我說，我自己面對我自己曾經做過的 自己心裏特別過意不去的 過不去這個坎兒 它這個背後的心性工夫是怎麽樣的？ 我怎麽跨過去的？ 我講了之後，大家聽了之後都很開心 還有呢？就是講這個儒家的禮 我們儒家是最講這個禮的 當時我面對的是這些美國的同學 大家都是有信仰的，講這個儒家的禮 美國人有壹個特點，大家都了解 有的時候學生上課，這個聽課 有翹二郎腿的，有直接盤腿在椅子上的 坐成什麽樣的都有 我說，大家都是有信仰的這些同學 我說，這個大家先別…… 我說，妳們現在坐 我們用儒家的這個正坐的這個姿勢 我給每個人調整了壹下 我說：妳當下是不是感覺 妳自己，連通妳自己的這個信仰 或者說妳自己當下的信仰體驗 更加地充分，更加地清晰了？ 大家都說“是” 結果我就從這開始講 這壹堂課下來，所有的人都保持這樣的壹個姿勢 什麽東西它都代替不了的是 當下人對於信仰，真實地這樣壹個體驗 就是工夫的問題 我在這方面也有壹些這種經歷吧 比如說我第壹次在神學院做這個禮 做這個祭孔的禮儀 當時所有的音樂呀這些，儀程我自己設計 我當時，向孔子行大禮、獻壹些祭品的時候 後來我跟朋友交流，他說： 妳在跪的時候，妳有壹個狀態起來 我說：妳說的對呀！妳怎麽知道？ 他說：我感覺到的 他是信基督教的，(對儒家)什麽都不了解 我跟他講說，禮和仁愛到底是什麽關系 他從那個角度說 那我現在壹下子明白 妳的禮當時都是怎麽做的了 他不懂儒家，也不懂什麽儒門工夫 他都不知道 他就坐在那裏，看我行這個禮 他知道裏面壹些東西是怎麽回事 那是因為什麽？他自己有這方面的體驗 有這方面的修養 他知道什麽是行禮 美國的這種社會，或者美國人 他對儒家，尤其是對於當下中國的 認識的壹種轉變 我覺得是從他對於儒家的開始地了解 發生這種變化的 其中我自己很深的壹個印象是 我當時在南加州大學的時候 跟我們學校的牧師，分享了我自己 就是學習儒學，從工夫上這個體驗的時候 他就很感興趣，他就說： 儒家這麽好的這樣壹個精神信仰 發展的歷程是怎麽樣的？ 所以由這個我就跟他談到了中國的歷史 我覺得談到中國歷史的時候 很多問題他開始慢慢地理解和解開了 我就談，我說：儒家是怎麽被迫中斷的？ 這個中斷的時間在哪？ 就是 咱們通常在這個歷史當中談的這個“三重門” 滿清、新文化運動的這個極左的思潮 還有這個文革 當我把這個三次大的割裂 壹步壹步壹步談清的時候 他當時就有壹個感受 他就很支持我們做儒家 他談的壹個觀點是什麽？ 他說：非常可貴的是，他作為西方人 他之前沒能意識到的是 中國人本身有壹個自己的這個傳統 這個傳統是非常好的 它在正常發展的時候 不僅能夠支持社會的繁榮，中國社會的繁榮 他在今天也有益於社會 當今世界全人類的發展 也是因為這壹點 就是後來在我實踐儒學 在美國進行這種宗教的對話 儒學的傳播當中 他給予了我很大的這種幫助 我覺得這是壹個人 當他真正了解到儒家的這種高度 儒家的歷史之後 他對於當下現實的判斷 做出的壹種必然的壹種反應 他們有那麽多的誤解，不是他們的問題 是我們自己的歷史中斷了 那我們在今天 我自己感覺我們有義務講壹個 我們中國人真正認可的壹個歷史 尤其是儒家的這個歷史 給西方人了解 讓他們意識到，中國問題的這種深度 中國問題，它的這種 真正的這種原理是什麽？ 而且講中國，講咱們說的這個400年的歷史 其實不僅是在談壹種民族大義 就這個歷史為什麽會中斷？ 那個中斷那個點到底發生了什麽樣的壹個變化？ 其實對於我們“怎麽認識人” 就是對於整個世界有信仰的人 或者說這個宗教、信仰，它的發展 也有壹種很大的壹個啟發意義 這是算是壹個歷史的活生生的壹個教訓在那裏 所以把中國的這樣壹段歷史 尤其是我們從儒家的高度，把它講清楚 這對於整個世界信仰的這種發展 也會有很獨到的壹個意義 恐怕其他的信仰，也許不從我們儒家這樣的角度 來看待自己的歷史 我們今天呀 探討了 我們是儒者在美國，所見 所聞 所感 尤其呢，談到了目前 美國社會對儒家的這種看法 也談到了我們對未來儒家參與到 與世界宗教、各種信仰對話的可能性的壹種期待 這不是說我們儒家要把自己宣傳出去 這樣的壹種很狹隘的壹種觀念，而是說 在這樣的壹種願望下 儒家有它自己的聲音、有它自己的高度 也可能產生它自己的貢獻 這件事呢，可能是壹件大事 我們的聲音是非常細弱的壹點聲音 但我們是壹個開始

  8. @lancialonginus: 孟子云「有諸己之謂信」。中國的，或曰儒家的信仰，是自信。以「自己」爲萬物的本體。

人者，仁也。人二會意，是對他人，進而萬物感同身受的體驗。正因人所謂「自己」這一體驗的內涵，即是感知的對象，所以物我是一。陽明所言花樹之喻，便是此義。而孟子也因此言「萬物皆備於我」。

萬物皆爲「我」之內涵，則「我」即可謂是一容器，「我」因此也即是太虛。

- This Taoist Concept Changed My Life Forever – Wu Wei Explained: Kind: captions Language: en [Music] Today we continue reflecting on a profound concept from tauist philosophy. Wouei action through non-action. It's a challenging idea because it seems counterintuitive. How do you act by not acting? How do you let go without neglecting responsibility? We live in a world that constantly demands action. Society teaches us to push, strive, and control outcomes. But Taoism suggests a different path. Act with minimal effort yet achieve maximum results. This doesn't mean passivity or avoidance. Instead, it's about aligned action. Doing what's essential and nothing more. Take for example the burden of debt. People often obsess over it. losing sleep and straining relationships. Yet, paradoxically, such fixation only worsens the problem. Letting go doesn't mean ignoring the debt, but rather addressing it with focused and intentional action while releasing the emotional weight of control. The difficulty lies in the ego's need for control. The ego resists the idea of surrendering outcomes. It craves certainty and immediate solutions. However, true peace comes from embracing the flow of life. Much like nature operates effortlessly. To internalize this, one must cultivate inner silence. This is where meditation plays a pivotal role. Through meditation, we quiet the mind, detach from the ego's grasp, and connect to the divine flow. Here's a key distinction. Trust differs from hope. While hope implies uncertainty, trust is rooted in certainty and confidence. When we trust the process, we act with conviction, knowing that the best outcome will unfold naturally. So I invite you to reflect. Identify a challenge in your life where you feel stuck or overwhelmed. Consider how many unnecessary actions drain your energy without progress. Now practice wooi. Focus on the essential. Act precisely and confidently and release your attachment to controlling the outcome. Let life flow naturally and watch as peace and clarity replace struggle and frustration. Together we can walk this path of growth and wisdom, embracing a life of balance and harmony.

- Wu-wei | The Art of Letting Things Happen: Kind: captions Language: en Once upon a time, a novice farmer indulged himself in motivational videos. He became familiar with ideas like the importance of ‘effort,’ the ‘hustle culture,’ and ‘work hard, play hard.’ After binge-watching for days, he walked onto his farm, fired up, and determined to make it a great success. He started with pulling one of the crops as a way to make them grow faster. But this didn’t work. Then he began to water the plants twice as much, hoping to make them grow faster, but he drowned them instead. After trying to force his success, the farmer realized that no matter how much effort he puts in, acting in opposition to nature is counterproductive. Despite humanity’s technological developments and will to progress, we’re still utterly dependent on nature. Human effort has its limitations and is always in conjunction with nature. We cannot grow a plant, for example, completely isolated from natural growth, even though we can influence and manipulate it. The reputed author of the Tao Te Ching, Lao Tzu, stated that the world governs itself. It doesn’t need our intervention. As Lao Tzu wrote: “When you arrive at non-action, nothing will be left undone.” End quote. This idea resembles the basic understanding of the paradoxical concept “wu-wei.” But when we look for a definition of wu-wei, we quickly discover that there isn’t one fixed meaning. Some translate wu-wei as “non-action,” or “doing nothing,” others as “actionless action,” and others as “effortless action.” We find out that Taoism offers several layers, if you will, regarding how wu-wei can enhance our relationship with the world. And how this ancient art of “letting things happen” doesn’t necessarily make us passive, ignorant bystanders but can actually improve how we act, leading to better outcomes. This video serves as a humble attempt to make the depth of this philosophical idea clear and practical. Probably the most common interpretation of wu-wei is quite literally aligning our actions with nature, and not forcing anything, and acting when it’s suitable. Lao Tzu stated that using force always leads to unseen troubles. With force, he doesn’t seem to point to the literal use of force per se, but rather to “forcing the flow of nature.” Nature has its course. It’s always working in the background. It lies at the root of our humanity’s existence and continually creates and recreates what the Taoists call the 10,000 things: everything under heaven and beyond. But despite the immense propulsion of the universe, today’s society places the importance of conscious human effort above all. We celebrate effort, regardless of its actual effectiveness, as we aim to always be busy for the sake of being busy. As modern Sisyphuses, we push rocks uphill, just for the sake of pushing. Making an effort is one thing, but intelligently making an effort is another. Yes, sometimes hard work is necessary, and action is appropriate, but on many occasions, it isn’t. It depends on the rhythm of the game, on the cards you’re dealt, and whether or not it’s your turn. Acting out of turn means going against the flow of the game. Hence, we often experience that “forcing things” leads to ruin in the long run and that pushing our luck beyond nature’s constraints backfires. On the other hand, situations often solve themselves when we don’t force or intervene. By letting things happen, things get done naturally. The novice farmer, for example, simply needed to leave his crops alone and be patient, and nature would do the rest. But when it’s time to reap the farmer should roll up his sleeves. “The Tao never acts with force, yet there is nothing that it can not do,” Lao Tzu stated. If we’d just go along with nature, then everything runs smoothly. The farmer’s task is to sow the seeds, the seeds’ task is to grow into crops, and then the farmer’s task is to reap the harvests when they’re ready. This is how nature works optimally. Lao Tzu believed that the world cannot be governed, as the world governs itself. It’s entirely out of control. Trying to control it is always a losing battle. And spending our lives fighting against nature is a waste of time, no matter how much people celebrate us for engaging in such a heroic endeavor. Instead of spending our life energy trying to control, govern, and change the natural course of things, we’re better off accepting what is and navigating through it like water flowing through a rocky surface towards the ocean. I quote: Some are meant to lead, and others are meant to follow; Some must always strain, and others have an easy time; Some are naturally big and strong, and others will always be small; Some will be protected and nurtured, and others will meet with destruction. The Master accepts things as they are, and out of compassion avoids extravagance, excess and the extremes. When we go deeper into the meaning of wu-wei, it becomes even more intriguing. Let’s assume its most accurate definition is “effortless action.” As opposed to the conventional translations like “non-action” and “doing nothing,” it points to being active and on the move, even though the common denominator stands firm, namely: “letting things happen.” With effortless action, we could say that we’re not so much “doing” but rather “going.” We embark on a river stream without considering where it brings us: we’re setting sail into uncharted territory, but don’t let the great unknown faze us. We swim along with the current, not against it, making our way smooth, devoid of stammers and hiccups. Swimming against the current takes effort; so does holding onto a rock. When we force things, problems arise. An example is going on a date. The person you’re going to meet is interested in one thing only: getting to know you: the person you are, at that moment. But many people overprepare, overanalyze, and rehearse what they’re going to say. And so, the nerves come in; people overcompensate, are disingenuous, and come across as staged. Why is this the case? Most likely because we focus too much on the results. Zhuangzi talks about how different prospects can influence our actions and emotional states if we attach ourselves to them. A previous video named ‘Win Without Trying’ explains this in more detail, using Zhuangzi’s parable of the archer. In the context of a date, most of us want the date to become a success; we want the other person to like us. So, there’s pressure involved; there’s a price to be won, and if we don’t get it, we feel miserable. And thus, what could have been a fun, joyful night out, becomes a strenuous, forced, stressful event, with lots of discursive thoughts and mental preparations in advance. We are standing in our own way, blocking the natural flow of the universe, which we cannot possibly control, no matter how much thought and planning we throw at it. But imagine that you enter a date utterly unprepared, without even seeing it coming. Now you’re acting natural, and in the moment, and spontaneously, as you haven’t had the opportunity to overthink. There has been no time for planning, plotting, analyzing, and all that remains is a pure you: present and one with the moment. And most people love that. So, if we manage to let go of the results, we can act more effortlessly and responsively, just for the act itself. If we go even deeper, we arrive at the ego and emptiness. Because of past experiences, social conditioning, or ideologies, we have molded ourselves into knowledgeable, cultured human beings that fit the context they’re in. This context could be a religious community, a country, or a profession. We generally see this as a good thing. But there’s a downside. And this downside has to do with the ego. The ego often distorts how we perceive the reality of the world. When we approach a situation, for example, a conversation with a spouse, the ego tends to fill in the unknown gaps, with all kinds of prejudices, fantasies, and convictions. The larger the ego, the more it will dominate the situation, subsequently closing us off from what’s truly going on. Therefore, people with strong convictions experience great difficulty seeing the world in another light - or outside the confines of their intellectual prison. Hence, Lao Tzu urges us to return to a more unprocessed state, which he calls “the uncarved block.” We could see the uncarved block as a person who’s unencumbered by all kinds of intellectual baggage, so his vision of the world becomes clear. Dr. Woei-Lien Chong, a Dutch philosopher, and sinologist, explains her interpretation of wu-wei in a Dutch book about Taoist philosophy, emphasizing the element of emptiness or ‘openness.’ She describes the essence of practicing wu-wei as follows, and I quote: “And so the whole practitioner is able to, from the clarity of his egoless center, observe all relevant forces in a certain situation in their being, undistorted, so he can respond to them flawlessly.” So, if we look at wu-wei this way, it’s not just letting go, going along with the flow of nature, allowing the universe to do its job: it’s a much bolder move, for which we need a lot of courage. We have to be brave enough to let go of what we’ve learned, throw our normative frameworks in the trash, and flush our assumptions through the toilet. Or how Lao Tzu put it, and I quote: Renounce knowledge and your problems will end. What is the difference between yes and no? What is the difference between good and evil? Must you fear what others fear? Nonsense, look how far you have missed the mark! Zhuangzi tells us a story about a conversation between Confucius and one of his pupils: Yen hui. Yen Hui wanted to travel to the kingdom of Wei after he heard about its many problems and tyrannical ruler. So, he planned to use his knowledge and wisdom to lecture the king of Wei and steer the kingdom in the right direction. Confucius persuaded Yen Hui not to do it, telling him that his plans were too ego-driven and wouldn’t work. After all, no one is waiting for a know-it-all coming out of nowhere, telling you how to take care of your business, let alone a self-absorbed tyrant. But still, Confucius offered an alternative, which he called the ‘fasting of the heart.’ Confucius described the ‘fasting of the heart’ as a form of meditation, which leads to letting our preconceptions go and making room to receive. With an open and receptive attitude, Yen Hui would learn about Wei and its king, their customs and beliefs, the situation as it plays out, and eventually be much more capable of lending a helping hand. In this understanding of wu-wei, we let go of the ego, so we act in accordance with the situation at hand, as it is. We let things happen and act responsively, unrestrained by our limited, preconceived ideas. Or, as Lao Tzu put it: we return to being uncarved blocks. Chong stated, and I quote: No matter if it’s about politics, governance, diplomacy, or any other activity: what Zhuangzi calls wu-wei is this ultimate receptive and responsive way of action, from a crystal clear openness in yourself, one with the breathing of the cosmos. Thank you for watching.

就在你後背接觸到沙發 全身癱軟的那一到兩秒鐘裡 你的大腦會出現一種極其特殊的狀態 那是一種所有的焦慮、對明天的算計、 對外維持的完美人設 在瞬間全數脫落的感覺 你的腦海裡呈現出一種萬念俱寂的空白 但同時 你的意識又是無比清明、靈光獨耀的 你並沒有昏睡過去 你只是單純的存在著 這個奇妙的瞬間 原來就是黃真人所說的玄關 他不是一個空間位置 而是一個生命狀態 但這個狀態通常只有一兩秒 馬上腦子又開始想明天要吃什麼 報告還沒寫完 為了讓你能在日常中 主動契入並維持這個狀態 黃真人傳授了極具實操價值的兩句口訣 哪兩句？ 第一句是念起是病 不續即藥 這八個字可以說是對治現代焦慮的終極解藥 念起是病 實際應用起來 就是當你察覺到自己又開始為了一封 還沒寄出的電子郵件感到焦慮 或者腦海裡不斷重演白天和同事的爭執時 你不需要用力去壓抑或消滅這個念頭 你只要察覺到他升起了 然後選擇不跟隨 不去為這個念頭編造後續的劇情 這個清明、不評判的察覺本身 就是化解焦慮的藥 這真的非常實用 這就自然銜接到他的第二句口訣 放下屠刀 立地成佛 在內丹學的語境裡 這裡的屠刀指的應該不是實體的武器吧？ 當然不是 他指的是你大腦裡 那些將完整現實切割得四分五裂的紛擾雜念 也就是那些不斷分析、批判的念頭 當你被這些念頭困住時 你突然憑藉著那股清明的覺知從中一覺而出 就像是從一場荒誕的夢境中瞬間醒來 看著剛才還深陷其中的混亂 這道不受污染的覺照之光 就是帶你回到平靜彼岸的力量 這等於是把修行的門檻從深山老林 直接拉回到了你的辦公桌前、 你的通勤列車上 每一次情緒的波動 每一次雜念的升起 都是你練習一覺而出的絕佳機會 不過這也帶來了一個許多人在練習 正念或冥想時最容易踩進去的陷阱 什麼陷阱？ 當你真的在沙發上或者在呼吸練習中 抓到了那個清淨無念的狀態時 你的大腦會本能地想要去抓住他 維持他 啊對 會覺得哇我現在好平靜 我要一直保持這樣 不能破功 黃真人對這種心態有一個非常傳神的形容 叫做床上安床 動中尋動 靜裡求靜 床上安床 這個比喻極其精準 你本來已經躺在床上休息了 卻因為害怕睡不好 硬要在床上再架一張床來躺 這完全是畫蛇添足 黃真人用另一個太空與雲翳的意象 進一步剖析了這種焦慮 我們的心性本來就像廣闊的太空 當經歷了大休歇 雜念的雲朵散去 晴空萬里的狀態自然就顯現了 但人類的控制慾太強了 我們因為害怕失去這份清靜 開始在心裡暗自使勁 默念著我要保持這個安靜的狀態 千萬別讓雜念跑出來 這種刻意去守護清淨的念頭 其實就像是你自己抓了一把煙塵 撒向剛放晴的天空 你那層試圖控制你意的心思 反而讓原本廣闊無雲的心境變得狹窄逼仄 這就是為什麼很多人越是努力想要放鬆 反而覺得緊繃的原因 那針對這種狀況 黃真人有給出什麼具體的解法嗎？ 他給出了一套乾淨利落、 毫無造作的四步實操指南 我們一步一步來看 第一步是什麼？ 第一 當玄關還沒開 也就是你還沒進入那種極度清明的狀態時 你要做的只是順其自然地看著自己 不要帶著強烈的目的性去尋找 不要硬找 那第二步呢？ 第二 當你幸運的進入狀態了 玄關開啟時 你只需要安安穩穩的待在那裡 絕對不要加上一絲一毫刻意維持的思慮 就在那裡就好 第三步我覺得是直指人心的 如果在狀態中雜念不可避免的跑出來了 黃真人的指示是四個字 收回即是 你不需要自責 不需要懊惱自己定力不夠 只需要像收回釣魚線一樣 輕輕的把注意力拉回來就好 那如果想睡覺怎麼辦？ 很多人靜坐到最後會打瞌睡 這就是第四步 如果你在靜坐或放鬆時覺得昏昏欲睡 他的解法是 整頓即是 稍微調整一下姿勢 讓精神微微提振 不需要像警報器響了一樣猛然驚醒自己 這整套方法的運作核心 其實都在於保持那顆覺照之心 這顆心就像懸掛在天空中的太陽 保持著光明與洞察 只要來了一個雜念 你一覺而動即便是掃除 不留任何痕跡 不需要繁瑣的儀式 不需要強烈的意念 這才是最頂級的內心修煉 把心法梳理清楚後 我們必須來拆解內丹學裡 另一個常被嚴重誤解、 充滿神秘色彩的核心概念 你是說金丹嗎？ 金丹 太多武俠小說或通俗文化 把金丹描繪成一顆在肚子裡發光 甚至可以吐出來的實體珠子 既然我們今天是深度解析 我們必須還原它純粹的本貌 丹絕對不是物理意義上的重金屬結塊 黃真人給出了一個極具科學精神的定義 他說丹是丹田中生機勃勃的氤氳元氣 與不二元神的完美結合 這兩個專業術語有點深 我們來把它拆解開來 氤氳元氣可以理解為生命最原始、 最底層的純粹能量與活力 它是構成我們肉身運作的基礎 而不二元神 正是我們剛才反覆討論的 那種不帶任何評判、清明不昧的純粹覺知力 當那個生命能量與清明覺知徹底合而為一 毫無縫隙時 你就能回到一種 不受後天條件制約的先天狀態 這在內丹學裡 《失道而後德，失德而後仁，失仁而後義，失義而後禮。夫禮者，忠信之薄而亂之首》

在太平盛世的社會，人人都求道修道。這社會根本不用人管就很平安。因為人們都發心〈奉獻〉〈付出〉。也没有權利名相的追求與爭執。這是ㄑ有道〉的最高境界。
〈德〉的社會，開始有名相分別。但社會尚屬平安。
〈仁〉的社會，因為有可憐的人，才有慈悲者出現。
〈義〉的社會。人們在競爭演化中，開始産生弱肉強食現象。大欺小，強欺弱。所以有〈義者〉打抱不平。
〈禮〉的社會，人人都刁鑽於法律規條。公德心已不見。只有自私的爭奪。對上不忠，對他人不義不信。所以是〈忠信之薄而亂之首〉。……緊接着亂世末劫就來了。

- 道德經 第二章_老子 I Chinese poetry: Kind: captions Language: en Dao De Jing Chapter 2 All the world knows beauty, but when this arises, ugliness is born. All the world knows good, but when this arises, evil is born. Thus being and non-being produce each other, Difficult and easy complement each other, Long and short define each other, High and low oppose each other, Note and sound harmonize with each other, Before and after follow each other. Therefore, the Sage abides in non-action (wu-wei) and practices the teaching that has no words. The myriad things arise but she does not possess them, They come into being but she does not claim them. She works without relying on what she does, She accomplishes without dwelling on her achievements. Because she does not dwell on them, it is not that they leave her.

- 道德經 第37~45章 原文和解釋 Tao Te Ching Part 5: Kind: captions Language: zh 道常無為而無不為。侯王若能守之,萬物將自化。化而欲作,吾將鎮之以無名之樸。鎮之以無名之樸,夫󠇡將不欲。不欲以靜,天下將自定。 上德不德,是以有德;下德不失德,是以無德。上德無為而無以為;下德無為而有以為。上仁為之而無以為;上義為之而有以為。上禮為之而莫之應, 則攘臂而扔之。故失道而後德,失德而後仁,失仁而後義,失義而後禮。夫󠇡禮者,忠信之薄,而亂之首。前識者,道之華,而愚之始。 是以大丈夫處其厚,不居其薄;處其實,不居其華。故去彼取此。 昔之得一者:天得一以清;地得一以寧;神得一以靈;谷得一以盈;萬物得一以生;侯王得一以為天下正。其致之也,謂天無以清,將恐裂;地無以寧,將恐廢; 神無以靈,將恐歇;谷無以盈,將恐竭;萬物無以生,將恐滅;侯王無以為貞,將恐蹶。故貴以賤為本,高以下為基。 是以侯王自稱孤、寡、不穀。此非以賤為本耶?非乎?故致譽無譽。是故不欲琭琭如玉,珞珞如石。 反者道之動;弱者道之用。天下萬物生於有,有生於無。 上士聞道,勤而行之;中士聞道,若存若亡;下士聞道,大笑之。不笑不足以為道。故建言有之:明道若昧;進道若退;夷道若纇;上德若谷;大白若辱; 廣德若不足;建德若偷;質真若渝;大方無隅;大器晚成;大音希聲;大象無形;道隱無名。夫󠇡唯道,善貸且成。 道生一,一生二,二生三,三生萬物。萬物負陰而抱陽,沖氣以為和。人之所惡󠇡,唯孤、寡、不穀,而王公以為稱。故物或損之而益, 或益之而損。人之所教,我亦教之。強梁者不得其死,吾將以為教父。 天下之至柔,馳騁天下之至堅。無有入無間,吾是以知無為之有益。不言之教,無為之益,天下希及之。 名與身孰親?身與貨孰多?得與亡孰病?甚愛必大費;多藏必厚亡。故知足不辱,知止不殆,可以長久。 大成若缺,其用不弊。大盈若沖,其用不窮。大直若屈,大巧若拙,大辯若訥。靜勝躁寒勝熱。清靜為天下正。

- 【洞見無界】誤解了2000年？老子《道德經》第36章根本不是權謀！揭開隱藏千年的「逆轉生死」生命煉金術: Kind: captions Language: zh-Hant 歡迎收聽洞見無界 在這個充滿雜訊的時代裡 我們為你攤開厚重的文獻與筆記 帶你直擊那些最能顛覆認知的核心洞見 如果你現在正在通勤 或是剛好有一段屬於自己的安靜時光 請跟著我們一起 把思緒稍微沉澱下來 是的 歡迎大家 其實 我們已經陪著大家 走過了道德經的前三十五章 而今天我們要正式踏入第三十六章的領域 不過 我要先給大家打個預防針 這一次我們要看的視角 絕對會把你過去對這段文字的理解 整個徹底翻轉過來 沒錯 我覺得我們應該先回到源頭 讓大家聽聽這段兩千多年來 被無數人反覆咀嚼的原文 經文是這樣說的：「將欲噏之 必固張之； 將欲弱之 必固強之； 將欲廢之 必固興之； 將欲奪之 必固與之 是謂微明 柔勝剛 弱勝強 魚不可脫於淵 國之利器不可以示人」 呼 說實話 我第一次讀到這段筆記的時候 我心裡的第一個反應是 等等 老子這是在教我們怎麼玩弄權謀嗎？ 哈哈哈 很多人都有這個感覺 對啊你看字面上的意思嘛 想要收斂它就先故意擴張它 想要削弱它就先故意增強它 這聽起來 完全就像是一個城府極深的謀略家在說話啊 確實很像 比如在商戰裡 你想摧毀一個競爭對手你就先故意捧殺他 讓他驕傲 讓他無限膨脹 然後趁他鬆懈的時候一舉殲滅 呃這難道不是一種很世故很腹黑的算計嗎？ 如果你只停留在這個層面 用世俗權謀的濾鏡來看待這段話 那真的就是把這部 直指生命本源的經典給完全看低了 喔？ 所以不是我們想的那樣嗎？ 完全不是 我們今天手邊最核心的文獻 是清代修道大德 黃元吉先生的《道德經講義》。 透過他極其深刻的拆解 你會發現這段話裡完全沒有對外的陰謀 沒有什麼辦公室政治的算計 那老子到底在談什麼？ 他談的是一套完全向內的「生命煉金術」。 老子在這裡談的是內丹 是修行 甚至可以說 是一套逆轉生死的終極天機 逆轉生死？ 哇這個跨度真的有點大耶 如果不是用來對付外在的敵人 那是用來對付誰？ 我們得先搞清楚 為什麼生命會需要被逆轉？ 這個問題問得非常好 在黃元吉先生的這份講義裡 他反覆提到一個概念 叫做「凡夫的氣數」。 氣數？ 聽起來像是算命的用語 其實不是算命 所謂的氣數 就是宇宙間最無情但也最公平的自然規律 怎麼說？ 你看 天地有盈虛消長 人有壽夭窮通 春夏的時候萬物生發 到了秋冬就必然凋零 我們人類的肉身也是一樣的 從青春強壯 一步步走向細胞的衰敗與死亡 這就是生老病死嘛 沒人能躲得過 對 想像一條奔流向下的浩蕩大河 對於絕大多數沒有經過修煉的普通人 也就是文獻裡說的凡夫 你只能被這股洪流推著走 你順流而下 毫無還手之力 這就是順應自然陰陽的衰亡過程 就像秋天的落葉 時間到了 水分乾了 就只能無奈地掉下來 可是 既然這是宇宙的自然規律 那修行的人難道就能免疫嗎？ 黃元吉先生是怎麼看待這件事的？ 這就是修行者與凡夫最大的分水嶺了 黃元吉先生提出了一個非常震撼的觀點 叫做「逆施造化」。 逆施造化？ 如果凡夫是順流而下 那真正的修行就是一場挽回天地、 扭轉乾坤的浩大動作 是一場逆流而上的奮戰 所以這不是順其自然？ 在某個層次上是順應更高的自然 但對抗的是肉身衰亡的自然 我們把這個概念 套回你剛剛覺得很像權謀的那段經文： 「將欲噏之， 必固張之」 這裡的「之」， 如果不是指敵人 是指什麼？ 指的就是你自己的身體 還有你的生命力 所以不是去擴張敵人的慾望 那我是要擴張什麼？ 是在你的生命力即將衰退的時候 重新在體內喚醒生機 我們來描繪一幅內在的畫面 你想像一下 好 我試著想像 當外界到了秋天 或者對應到我們人步入中老年 你的身體氣血開始枯竭 開始收斂 這就是經文裡的「噏」， 還有「將欲廢之」的「廢」。 嗯 就是那種體力大不如前 感覺身體機能要報廢的感覺 歲月無情地奪走你的健康 這就是「將欲奪之」的「奪」。 在這個生命即將枯槁的嚴冬裡 真正的修行者 不是去外面算計別人 而是在自己體內 張天地之氣運 強血氣之功能 哇 在你最衰弱 看似即將熄滅的時候 硬是從體內升起一股勃勃的生機 這個視角的切換真的讓我起雞皮疙瘩耶！ 所以老子這是在教我們 當生命面臨危機和衰退的時候 如何對自己「易危為安 反亂為治」？ 這根本是一場對內部的生命重塑工程啊 一點也沒錯 那這就是經文接著說的「是謂微明」嗎？ 就是一種普通人看不見 但修行者自己心裡非常清楚的內在光明 完全正確 這是一種極其幽微 卻又真實存在的內在轉化 大家在外面看你 可能覺得你還是個普通人 但在你的內在 已經發生了翻天覆地的變化 真的很有畫面感 這就帶來了一個很容易讓人誤解的地方 也是很多人 在看這些丹道文獻時最常踩到的陷阱 什麼陷阱？ 你想想 既然我們要逆天改命 要對抗這麼強大的衰老洪流 直覺上我們是不是得用盡洪荒之力？ 絕對是啊 如果我的身體正在走向衰老 我現在的直覺反應就是去健身房硬碰硬 舉很重的啞鈴 或者如果是修行 我就會用意志力死命硬撐著去打坐 咬牙切齒地去搬運體內的氣血 逆流而上嘛 聽起來就需要極大的力氣和堅持啊 這恰恰是黃元吉先生極力要打破的迷思 你看老子緊接著給出的方法論是什麼？ 「柔勝剛 弱勝強」 柔弱？ 這怎麼對抗衰老？ 這就是道家最精妙的地方 這種逆轉生死的返還之術 靠的絕不是你後天的蠻力 如果你身體緊繃著去靜坐 用意念強行去引導氣血 那你永遠、永遠摸不到大道的邊緣 所以用力反而是錯的？ 大錯特錯 你必須做到極度的柔軟、極度的放鬆與虛靜 你要讓自己的身心頻率降到最低 才能接通宇宙中最強大的先天能量 這其實有點違背我們的直覺 我們從小被教導的就是要努力、要拼搏 為了讓我們具象化這種「柔弱」， 文獻裡老子拋出了一個千古隱喻 叫做「魚不可脫於淵」。 魚不能離開深潭 如果我們把人類比喻成這條魚 那維持我們生命的那個水、那個深淵 到底是什麼？ 我猜這應該不是指我們每天要喝滿八杯水 或者什麼物質層面的東西吧？ 你點出了關鍵 但歷史上真的有非常多人 往物質層面去鑽牛角尖 真的假的？ 真的 黃元吉先生在講義裡 非常嚴厲地批評了那些旁門左道 很多人誤以為這個水是有形有質的體液 像是口水之類的？ 或者是坊間房中術追求的那種有形的濁精 他們以為靠吞服某些物質 或者強留體液就能長生不老 黃元吉先生說這完全是南轅北轍 甚至是可笑的 那如果不是物質上的東西 我們真正的淵究竟在哪裡？ 我們該去哪裡找這深淵之水來滋養自己？ 真正的深淵之水 黃元吉先生稱之為「真一之精」。 真一之精？ 它絕對不是你吃什麼昂貴的冬蟲夏草、 人蔘能補出來的 我們用一個現代一點的比喻來說好了 現代比喻比較好懂 它不像是一支沒電的手機 你拿個行動電源從外面插上去充電 它比較像是在你把手機徹底關機 停止一切外部運作的那個絕對零度裡 手機內部某個神秘的自體發電機能 能夠被重新啟動 源源不絕地產生最純淨的能量 自體發電 文獻上說 這股能量是從「恍惚杳冥 虛無自然中生出來的」。 恍惚杳冥 這四個字聽起來很美 但真的很抽象耶 那到底是一種什麼樣的狀態？ 是像我們平常工作累了 坐在沙發上發呆那樣嗎？ 比發呆深沉太多了 發呆的時候你腦子裡 其實還是有微細的念頭的 也是 還會想著等一下要吃什麼 對啊 當你在靜坐時 要達到恍惚杳冥 代表你腦海裡那些明天工作怎麼辦、 剛才誰惹我生氣的雜念全都被剝離了 甚至到了最後 連我在靜坐、 我想要變健康的這個自我意識都融化了 連「我」都沒有了 就在那個極度空無、 你幾乎忘記自己存在的瞬間 宇宙本源的那股「一元之水」 才會像深山裡的湧泉一樣 汩汩湧出 灌溉你的身心 難怪 難怪我們準備的資料裡 特別引用了莊子的那句名言 「相濡以沫，不如相忘於江湖」。 如果放在這個語境下來看 這個畫面的對比實在太強烈、太有衝擊力了 你可以幫聽眾描繪一下這個畫面嗎？ 好啊你看喔 我們普通人 每天焦慮地尋找各種外在的養生法 吃各種維他命做醫美 其實就像是兩條被困在乾涸車轍裡的魚 快要渴死了 我們快渴死了 只能靠吐口水互相濕潤對方苟延殘喘 活得超級辛苦又很可悲 是啊 這就是凡夫的狀態 而真正的修行者 他們根本不屑於那一點點口水 他們是不依賴外在物質的 他們去哪了？ 他們是直接躍入大道所化成的江湖 在極度的寧靜中 連我都忘記了 讓整個宇宙的深淵之水來無條件地滋養自己 這才是「魚不可脫於淵」的真諦 呼……好的 那我們現在知道目標了 目標就是那股從虛無中湧出的真一之精 但說實話 這到底要怎麼做到啊？ 我看著資料裡 接下來出現的這幾個內丹學術語 我真的覺得有點頭皮發麻 各位聽眾 你們聽聽看這兩個詞喔 「於黑山窟裡取陽」、「鬼窩裡取寶」。 這名字確實很嚇人 黑山窟、鬼窩 這聽起來簡直像是在看什麼奇幻盜墓小說 我們不是在談高雅的道德經嗎？ 這到底是什麼意思啦？ 我必須說 這確實是這份文獻裡最震撼 也是整個內丹學最核心的高潮 所謂的黑山窟和鬼窩 其實是非常精妙的隱喻 所以不是真的要去什麼洞穴探險 當然不是 天地之間有生機也有殺機 當一個人在深度靜坐時 他必須先讓自己進入一種極度深沉、 陰寒、萬念俱死的地步。 萬念俱死 在那個狀態下 你的呼吸、你的脈搏都微弱到了極點 所有的慾望都停止運作 在凡人看來 這種狀態就像是死了一樣了無生趣 這就是絕對的陰 也就是文獻裡說的殺機 這就是所謂的黑山窟 等一下 這聽起來很危險啊 為了活得更好 為了得到生機 反而要先讓自己 進入一種接近死亡的殺機狀態 這這這其實有點像我們現代人 有時候工作壓力大到極點 徹底崩潰了 攤在沙發上什麼都不想管、 什麼都放棄的那個瞬間 這個聯想滿有意思的 只說修行者是主動 而且非常清醒地進入這種自我死亡的狀態 關鍵就在於主動與清醒 當你徹底放棄了那個嘈雜的小我 你不掙扎了 你安然地進入了極陰的鬼窩之中時 宇宙的規律會發生什麼事？ 物極必反 答對了 至陰赫赫 至陽肅肅 就在你念頭完全死寂的那一剎那 突然之間會有一點純陽的生機 也就是內丹學裡說的「一陽來復」， 會從虛無中猛烈地爆發出來 就像是在最黑的深淵裡 突然劃破天際的一道閃電 就是那個瞬間 而就在這純陽之氣爆發的千鈞一髮之際 修行者要在虛空中 一把將這股強大的能量奪過來 收歸己有 奪過來 這在丹道裡有一個非常專門的術語 就叫做「盜機」。 盜機？ 偷盜的盜 機制的機 我的天啊 這個動詞用得太傳神了吧！ 你看喔 我們普通人是被歲月、 放下刻意的痕跡 放下言語的瑕疵 放下死板的計算 放下內心的防備 放下對他人的評判 當你把這些都放下了 那份本自具足的道 就自然顯現了 所以今天留給我們聽眾的思考題 可能不是我該如何練習 而是在我現在的生活中 那個我一直忽略 甚至厭惡的人或事 會不會恰恰就是我最寶貴的老師和資糧 這確實是一個值得我們帶回去 好好琢磨的問題 感謝大家的聆聽 我們下期洞見無界再見 請記得訂閱洞見無界 與我們一同探索智慧的深處

- Wu Wei 無為 (Taoist Non-Action): Kind: captions Language: en In a previous video, you heard me say this: Because the West has got it wrong for far too long. I should not have said that, because it’s not true. I don’t think you can get it “wrong.” We each bring our lived experiences and cultural constructs, culturally conditioned ideologies to the table and interpret texts such as the Tao De Ching through our specific lens. There’s no wrong or right about it. Wu wei in a nutshell, according to Bell: Just because you can doesn’t mean you do. Do not persist against resistance. Walk the Path that you are naturally aligned to, and you will know what that Path is by way of intuition, your inner knowing. You were born knowing about your own nature, your own Purpose. So don’t fight it. But that’s enough from my perspective. This video is a presentation of what various authoritative “primary sources” (if you can call it that) had to say about wu wei. The seminal text to study for trying to understand wu wei would be the Tao Te Ching. And while I want to deep-dive into every single reference to wu wei in the 81 verses, I also don’t want to make a 3-hour long video. The term wu wei is referenced directly about 11 times, or 22 characters of the 5,000 character text. And you could write a whole dissertation trying to explain each one of these verses. So at the risk of being reductive, I’m only going to cover a few. The reference to wu wei found in Verse 2 is arguably one of the more important or at least more famous references. “The awakened ones adhere to wu wei, sensing and teaching through silence. The Myriad Beings come to be without defiance. To rise without possession.” In the orthodox ordering of the 81 verses, that is the first time the term wu wei comes up in the text. Wàn wù zuò yān ér bùcí. The passage underlined on screen, “The Myriad Beings come to be without defiance,” is a popular Chinese proverb that means the Tao does not interfere when there is injustice, disaster, or suffering. Why not? Because the Tao, in all its omnipotent, omniscient wisdom, knows that all transgressions shall meet with their due consequences. So the Tao as the divine monad in monotheism does not interfere with our suffering and tragedies. Our Creator lets nature take its course. Suggesting that, on the monotheistic level, the one god that is the Tao, Tian Di or Shang Di does not perform miracles. The Tao as Tian Di or Shang Di, the Divine Monad, does not engage in thaumaturgy. But fragments of the Tao, at the polytheistic level, certain gods, spirits, and even demons will supernaturally interfere with human events and the course of nature. The rest of that passage, shēng ér bù yǒu 生而不有, means, put crudely, something to the effect of you shouldn’t let your ambitions lead you to exploiting people, or exploiting nature, natural resources. Don’t force yourself to rise to the top. Rise to the top because you are naturally…the best. Meaning, instead of conceptualizing strength as exerting something physical and external, conceptualize strength as internal and unforced. That which is warm will naturally, by its own nature, rise above that which is cold. That which is “dense” or substantive, of higher mass per unit volume, rises to the top. In other words, wu wei means to rise above naturally, and not rise by pushing other people down. Verse 38 from the Tao Te Ching: High virtue is to embody wu wei and make no presumptions; Low virtue is to take action based on your presumptions. I think that is some seriously good advice, albeit difficult to follow… If we’re going to accept that the Tao Te Ching is authoritative on providing context for understanding wu wei, then it’s clear, within the text, that it doesn’t mean literally take no action. Dào cháng wú wéi ér wú bù wéi. 道常無為而無不為. Verse 37. The Path of the Tao is wu wei, non-action, and yet not no action. That translation requires a bit of explanation. So the enduring and constant path of the Tao, Dào Cháng 道常 is the path of wu wei non-action, meaning it IS the path of nature. We define wu wei as the ever enduring and constant path of the Tao. Wu wei is the path of the Tao. The path of the Tao is wu wei. But the Tao is in constant motion, an ever-enduring state of change, so it’s not that the Tao is not in movement. It’s motion with no resistance. It’s movement that is unforced. By the way, that line from verse 37 of the Tao Te Ching, Dào cháng wú wéi ér wú bù wéi, is referenced in all the popular videos here on YouTube on the subject of wu wei. On this Channel, you’ll see it on screen, along with the relevant time stamp, that line from Verse 37 is translated to “When you arrive at non-action, nothing will be left undone.” In this video on the subject, that same exact verse is translated to: “The Way never acts, yet nothing is left undone.” Again, time stamp and channel name on screen. And finally, this one, translated to “The Dao does not act, and yet there is nothing it does not do.” All great translations. And now it’s your turn to interpret that very important line for yourself, that defining explanation for wu wei from the Tao Te Ching. The Tao eternal, or the enduring Tao; the constant, the invariable wu wei, non-action. But “wéi” can mean the noun “being” and it can also indicate the verb “be.” So that’s confusing. “ér” is a particle indicating the relational connection between what came before and what is about to come after. same “wu” from wu wei as before. not, or the negation of, which lets us know that the previous particle “ér” is “but.” “wei” from wu wei, action, to do, to administer, to be, to behave as. And so, the Tao eternal or “The Tao enduring is non-action but not no action.” The Tao enduring is non-being but not no being. Wéi 為 translated to “action” of wu wei “non-action,” is actually a pretty tough word to translate completely.The character itself dates back to the Zhou dynasty oracle bone scripts. Its original indication was that of forced labor, indentured servitude, something manually done, almost with the meaning of “micro-managing” another. To exert forced control. To try to control. To try and embody something you’re not. To do something that deep down you don’t innately, by your nature, want to do. So wu wei 無為, in the negative, means the opposite of that. Now it’s for you to put that together and interpret what that line is telling you about wu wei. Journaling exercise. Start by taking each character piecemealed to understand the full range of its implications. Then synthesize these eight characters into a cohesive meaning. Filter that through your lived experiences, your way of thinking, even your spiritual path and values, and reflect on what this line is telling you about you. The Tao Te Ching is a historical text instructive on the moral and ethical way to govern a kingdom, how to be a model sovereign. So wu wei is the political philosophy and military strategy of minimal institutional interference.Do not act in a way that causes imbalances between heaven, nature, and humanity. So “non action” is in fact the affirmative act of maintaining equilibrium. You see this more explicitly stated in Verse 3: Action by non-action, for peace and order to prevail. Wéi wú wéi, zé wú bù zhì. 為無為，則無不治 Govern by way of non-action. Let nature take its course. Rule in such a way that allows people to be their true nature. The Xunzi 荀子, circa 475 to 221 BC, has this one passage in there that really explains wu wei quite well: Wu wei is the only way one can cultivate true knowing of the Mysteries. Wu wei is to see color through blindness and to hear sound through deafness. Another seminal text on understanding wu wei is the Zhuangzi 莊子, circa 350 BC to 250 BC. There are at least a dozen passages in the Zhuangzi that are on point with respect to discussion of wu wei. The most famous allegory that’s illustrative of the principle of wu wei is the story of a master butcher, and this particular story is quite counter-intuitive to the typical Western interpretation of wu wei non-action. A butcher slaughters an ox in one stroke, with no pain to the ox, and the way he does this appears so effortless and easy, as if it took no strength at all. The way the butcher achieves this is by knowing the “Path of the Tao” through the ox’s joints, knowing the path of least resistance. Which is why it didn’t require physical strength or force. The blade took the path of least resistance and the butcher possessed the mastery, the wisdom, and the skill to know what that path of least resistance was. That’s wu wei, according to the Zhuangzi. Now let’s fast forward a few centuries to the Western Han 西漢 dynasty. The Xīnyǔ 新語 is a Confucian text written around 196 BC that consists of 12 chapters, attributed to Lù Jiǎ 陸賈 a Taoist political thinker and philosopher. The founding emperor of the Han dynasty, Liú Bāng 劉邦, asked Lù Jiǎ to recite and summarize for the imperial court the lessons learned from the fall of the Qin dynasty, which Lù Jiǎ did in 12 articles that became known as the Xīnyǔ. For this discussion, we’re going to focus only on Article 4, titled Wú Wéi. Verse 1 of the Chapter on wu wei emphasizes the importance and the supremacy of the political and philosophical principle of non-action. Emperor Shun, who came before Yu the Great, along with the Duke of Zhou, one of the legendary co-authors of the I Ching, ruled the people by song and rites. Meaning, they themselves embodied the highest virtues, reverent to the Divine, reverent to knowledge, the arts, and humanities, thereby inspiring their people to be the same. The ideal form of rule is to be hands-off. Let the people be free to be themselves, and they will naturally aspire to be their higher selves. That’s… very idealistic. Xīn Yǔ is considered a Confucian text, and Confucianism is often described as idealistic. Confucianism is premised, fundamentally, on the belief that people are inherently good, that deep down, we humans want to be good, want to do good. And so the government’s role is to foster an environment that inspires people to live up to their innate goodness. Verse 2 reflects on political lessons learned from the terrifying Emperor Qin and why his ways of rule brought about his own dynasty’s downfall. Qin Shi Huang built the Great Wall of Imperial China because he ruled out of fear, and used fear to rule. He didn’t allow free-thinking and he oppressed his own people, hoarding wealth greedily for himself instead of distributing that wealth throughout the kingdom. When you fear chaos and disorder, you inadvertently produce and amplify chaos and disorder. The tighter you try to grip, the more you lose, the more it all slips through your fingers. Rule with fear and your own paranoia becomes justified, because you’ve inadvertently created something fearsome, something you now should fear. Be a monster, create a monster. One more verse from Xīn Yǔ before we move on. There’s a total of 5 verses in Article 4. Verse 3 references the jun zi, which you’ll recall is a recurring reference in the I Ching Book of Change. Jun zi is kinda like your higher self. What would the most exalted, the wisest version of you do? The concept here is that of the ideal human. Implied in the principle of wu wei non-action is to walk the Middle Path, to be at the center. A sage ruler cannot be partisan. The sage ruler must possess the capacity to truly empathize with both sides. Wu wei means to control by not being controlling. A wise king is going to embody the jun zi, the inner sage. According to Lu Jia’s philosophy and interpretation of wu wei, you don’t force people to be good through severe law and punishment. You inspire people to be good by exemplifying good yourself. In other words, lead by example, not by being a micro-managing tyrannical jerkface. This approach to interpreting wu wei checks out with the Tao Te Ching. Case in point. Verse 63: Wǒ wú wéi, ér mín zì huà 我無為，而民自化. That line is in the first person: “I embody wu wei, and so the people transform themselves.” The people self-enlighten. If the Tao is sentient and speaking in first person, they’re very optimistic and thinks quite highly of human nature. Just saying. Teach without lecturing, for wu wei begets the Yi. May all strive to attain this. Yi here is a reference to Hexagram 42 from the I Ching. Burgeoning. State of increase. To be on the ascent. Spiritual growth. Affluence. Abundance. Hexagram 42 contains that famous proverb: When sages see the good of others, they are inspired to be better. And when they see the fault of others, they correct that fault in themselves. When you embody the principle of wu wei, which is exemplified in the meaning of Hexagram 42 from the I Ching, you shall be blessed with gains and affluence, with abundance and growth, expansion, and enrichment. I think if your reaction to this moral philosophy of wu wei is that it sounds way too naïve and not starting with realistic presumptions of human nature, I’d say it’s a fair critique. Interestingly, around this time, between 200 and 100 BC, Stoicism was popular in ancient Greece. And specifically if you consider Diogenes of Babylon (230 – 140 BC), who lived ballpark around the time of Lu Jia, just… 5,000 miles west. Or 8,000 kilometers, give or take. In short summary, the Greek Stoic philosopher Diogenes of Babylon espoused virtue and wisdom as the highest good, that we should live in harmony with nature, embody a mindset of indifference, apatheia, in order to maintain inner peace, and to accept come what may in terms of your fate, embracing joy and challenge with equanimity. Which sounds a lot like the Taoist-Confucianist philosophy of wu wei as espoused by Lu Jia, same time period, different continent. The synchronicity of thought is really interesting. The moral philosophy of wu wei is embedded in Confucianism, Taoism, and Buddhism. The Confucianist perspective of wu wei is this: The one above must model exemplary behavior, which in turn inspires the below to freely, voluntarily, of their own volition embody that exemplary behavior. The Taoist perspective of wu wei: Let people be free to be themselves. Heaven lets Earth, or Nature, to be free to be itself. Earth, Nature, lets humans be free to be themselves. And that is The Way. Buddhist perspective of wu wei is non-attachment, to cultivate a state of liberation where you no longer crave or attach yourself to that which is impermanent – the Taoist concept of constant change. Wu wei through a Buddhist lens is equanimity – a state of mental calmness and balance, the Middle Path. Temperance and always finding your way back to the harmonious center. By a show of hands, how many of us are still Taoist after learning all this? Maybe a little bit too ambitious, optimistic, and love and light for me. But what say you? Reflecting on these historical texts directly, how do they speak to you? What’s your interpretation of wu wei? There’s no right or wrong. Only your lived experience, values, and learned wisdom. Be brave, speak your truth, leave your interpretation, contemplations, and thoughts on wu wei in the comments section.

- What Is Wu Wei? The Taoist Secret to Doing Less and Achieving More: Kind: captions Language: en You've been told your whole life that success is about doing more. More effort, more hustle, more optimization. And yet, the more you push, the more exhausted you become. The harder you try to force things, the more they seem to resist. What if the problem isn't that you're not doing enough? What if the problem is that you've been taught to fight the current when you could learn to move with it? Today, we're going to look at one of the oldest ideas in human philosophy. A concept from ancient China called wooi and why it might be the most radical, counterintuitive, and quietly life-changing thing you've never been taught. Here's the world most of us are living in. We're busier than any generation in history. And somehow we feel like we're falling behind. We wake up tired. We go to sleep with our mind still running. We fill every gap with stimulation because silence feels wrong, almost suspicious. Self-help culture tells us the answer is better habits, stronger discipline, a more optimized morning routine. And none of it quite works. Not really. Because almost all of it is built on the same assumption. That more effort is always the answer. That life is something to be conquered, not something to be navigated. Towism looked at this very differently. And its central teaching, Wooi, offers something that Western philosophy rarely does. Permission to stop fighting. Wooi literally means non-action or doing nothing. But that translation is almost always misunderstood. Wooi doesn't mean laziness. It doesn't mean passivity. Think of water. Water doesn't force its way through rock. It finds the path of least resistance. And through that effortless, patient movement, it carves canyons. The taqing puts it this way. The tow does nothing. Yet through it all, things are done. Wooue is the art of acting in accordance with the natural flow of things. Not forcing, not resisting, but responding. It's the difference between paddling frantically upstream and reading the current, letting it carry you, and using small precise movements to steer same destination. fraction of the effort. So what does this actually look like in your life right now? The first way woe shows up is in how you work. Most of us work in one of two modes. Either grinding through resistance or avoiding it entirely. Wooi suggests a third option. Finding the state where the work flows. Psychologists now call this flow state. Athletes call it being in the zone, but toists understood it 2500 years ago. The question Wooue asks is, "What would this look like if it weren't hard?" The second place woui appears is in relationships. will way in relationships means showing up as you are responding rather than performing and trusting the authentic presence has more pull than any constructed version of yourself. And the third perhaps the most radical is in how you respond to difficulty. Wooi suggests pausing, observing, asking what is the situation asking for rather than what am I trying to impose on it. Not pacivity attunement. So how do you actually practice wooi once a day just once when you feel yourself straining pushing or forcing something? Stop. Not forever just for a moment. Take three slow breaths and ask yourself a single question. Am I fighting the current right now or am I trying to navigate it? That question alone starts rewiring how you approach things because wooi is less a technique and more a listening practice. It asks you to become sensitive to resistance to notice when effort is productive and when it's just noise. Over time, you start to recognize the difference between the resistance that signals you're growing and the resistance that signals you're going the wrong way. Water knows this instinctively. We're just learning to remember it. Wooi isn't a philosophy of giving up. It's a philosophy of intelligent effort, of learning to work with the grain of life rather than constantly against it. It's one of the oldest ideas in the world. And in a culture that worships busyness, it might also be one of the most needed. Next week, we're going into Buddhist philosophy and the concept of impermanence and specifically why your attachment to your goals might actually be the thing slowing you down. If you want to think more slowly in a world that never stops, that's what this channel is for. Subscribe so you don't miss it. And if this video helped you see something a little differently today, share it with one person who might need to hear it. I'll see you next week.

- EASTERN PHILOSOPHY: Wu Wei: Kind: captions Language: en-GB 'Wú Wéi' (无为) means, in Chinese, 'non-doing', or 'doing nothing'. It sounds like a pleasant invitation to relax, or worse, fall into laziness or apathy. Yet this concept is key to the noblest kind of action, according to the philosophy of Daoism, and is at the heart of what it means to follow 'Dào' (道) - 'the way'. According to the central text of Daoism, the 'Dào Dé Jīng' (道德经),the way never acts,yet nothing is left undone. This is the paradox of Wú Wéi : it doesn't mean 'not acting,' it means 'effortless action,' or 'actionless action'. It means being at peace, while engaged in the most frenetic tasks, so that one can carry these out with maximum skill and efficiency. Something of the meaning of Wú Wéi is captured when we talk of being 'in the zone', at one with what we're doing, in a state of profound concentration and flow. Wú Wéi is closely connected to the Daoist reverence for the natural world, for it means striving to make our behaviour as spontaneous and inevitable as certain natural processes, and to ensure that we are swimming with, rather than against, currents. We are to be like the bamboo that bends in the wind, or the plant that adjusts itself to the shape of a tree. Wú Wéi involves letting go of ideals that we may otherwise try to force too violently onto things. It invites us instead to respond to the true demands of situations, which tend only to be noticed when we put our own ego-driven plans to one side. What can follow is a loss of self-consciousness, a new unity between the self and its environment. But none of this means we won't be able to change or affect things if we strive for Wú Wéi. The Dào Dé Jīng points out that we should be like water, which is submissive and weak, and yet which can't be surpassed for attacking what is hard and strong. Through gentle persistence and a compliance with the specific shape of a problem, an obstacle can be worked around and gradually eroded. The idea of achieving the greatest effects by wise, strategic passivity has been central to Chinese notions of politics, diplomacy and business. In the manuals on wisdom produced by Daoists, we are repeatedly told that rather than impose a plan or model on a situation, we should let others act frantically, and then likely adjust ourselves as we see the direction that matters have evolved in. In China's Táng (唐) dynasty, many poets likened Wú Wéi to the best aspects of being drunk. It wasn't alcoholism they were promoting, but the decline in rigidity and anxiety that sometimes comes with being a little drunk, and which can help us to accomplish certain tasks. One poet compared someone inspired by Wú Wéi to a drunk man who falls uninjured from a moving cart. Such is their spiritual momentum, that they are unaffected by accidents and misfortunes that might break those of a more controlled and controlling mindset. Theories of painting from the Táng period onwards made Wú Wéi central to artistic practice. Rather than labouriously attempting to reproduce nature as faithful copyists, the artist should find nature within themselves, and surrender to its calls. The painter's task is not to imitate the external surface of things, but to present the 'Guǐ (鬼)' or 'spirit' of things like mountains, trees, birds and rivers, by feeling some of this spirit in themselves, and then letting it 'flow out' through the brush, onto silk or paper. It followed that Daoist thinkers revered not just the finished work of art, but the act of painting itself. It considered artist studios as places for applied philosophy. The Táng dynasty poet, Fú Zài (符载), described a big party that had been thrown to witness the painter, Zhāng Zǎo (張璪), in action. 'Right in the middle of the room, he sat down with his legs spread out, took a deep breath and his inspiration began to issue forth. 'Those present were as startled as if lightning was shooting across the heavens, or a whirlwind was sweeping up into the sky. 'The ink seemed to be spitting from his flying brush. 'He clapped his hands with a cracking sound. 'Suddenly, strange shapes were born. 'When he'd finished, there stood pine trees, scaly and riven, crags steep and precipitous, clear water and turbulent clouds. 'He threw down his brush, got up and looked around in every direction. 'It seemed as if the sky had cleared after a storm, to reveal the true essence of ten thousand things.' Fú Zài added of Zhāng, whose works are sadly now lost, that he had 'left mere skill behind', and that his art was 'not painting', but 'the very Dao itself'. A good life could not be attained by Wú Wéi alone, but this Daoist concept captures a wisdom we may at times be in desperate need of : when we're in danger of damaging ourselves, through an overly stern and unyielding adherence to ideas which simply don't fit the demands of the world.

I don't know what's the use To hold water and food It was the people at that time The two most common uses for gourds So this story illustrates Keiko, who is dominated by cold cognition and rational thinking no creativity Zhuangzi immediately came up with a way He said you can tie a big gourd to your waist and go swimming. this story Talk to modern education experts What is the use of bricks often made by children? That test is exactly the same Conventional thinking can only think of conventional uses of bricks But some children But I can think of unconventional uses of bricks Modern psychological experiments have indeed found If you get drunk Turn off the cold cognitive system Thinking about problems based on thermal cognition It is possible to acquire more divergent thinking bring creativity So the idea of pretending to be dead is also very scientific. So how can we achieve this state of divergent thinking? Zhuangzi's method is to forget For example, swimming Zhuangzi specifically mentioned If this person can forget himself forget fear Then he can swim very well If you always think about yourself when swimming Maybe I won’t even be able to move. This method Especially suitable for athletes and actors to play But Sen Gelan said Zhuangzi also has loopholes Without presupposing any value judgment Then why do you Zhuangzi think that Cold cognition is not as good as hot cognition If all the truth is expressed in words Just put a label on it Then why are you, Zhuangzi? You have to say so many words to Keiko again? You are also putting labels on yourself Look at the four sages Confucius, Laozi, Mencius and Zhuangzi their highest goal All of them perfectly unify cold cognition and hot cognition reach a state of inaction But their routes are very different And they all have their own shortcomings So as modern people, we How should one achieve the state of inaction? The methods of the four sages above actually make sense. can help you achieve inaction Everyone should base their preferences on their own personality choose different methods and at different stages of life Different methods can also be used In the early stages of learning any skill We should use Confucius’ method Study hard and practice hard Engage in immersive educational experiences Strive to make habits become natural When doing specific things Especially doing big things that can affect others We should refer to Laozi’s thoughts Does not easily interfere with complex systems Develop artistic taste Improve moral standards We can use Mencius’ approach Find your shining point as a seed develop slowly If you are facing a huge pressure and particularly important work We should learn from Zhuangzi forget yourself Let thermal cognition guide us to perform at our best So according to the four plans given to us by the four leaders, under different circumstances in different ways Maybe we can also do nothing We have finished the book Wuwei at this point. Let’s summarize the three points of this book together: The first thing is to do nothing Rather than deliberately pursuing No need for conscious control It seems like you are doing something particularly relaxing The result is very natural to achieve a state of inaction Must go through hard practice The second is to achieve the state of inaction What you do must conform to a certain value system Tao and virtue taught by ancient Chinese sages The liberalism pursued by modern people They are all value systems that can help you achieve Wuwei Third, Confucius, Laozi, Mencius, and Zhuangzi Through the book Wuwei we can see Wuwei is not a philosophical concept game it's a real thing Want to achieve the state of doing nothing Just relying on understanding is not enough Must be trained using scientific methods Learn to control your cold and hot cognitive systems Okay, that’s it for this issue. See you next time a western scholar Using the latest research results from brain science Proposed an explanation for Zhuangzi's "inaction" It sounds a bit unreliable But when I carefully studied his theory I now feel that this is one of the explanations of "inaction" I have heard The most reliable one The name of the book we are going to talk about today is Wuwei The book is subtitled The Art and Science of Spontaneity The English name of this book is try not to try Literally translated, it means that those who try should not be too deliberate. The author is Professor Mori Gelan, a Canadian scholar Sen Gelan is his Chinese name Mori Gelan is a famous Canadian sinologist And a PhD in Religion from Stanford University Focus on ancient Chinese thought He once translated the Analects of Confucius into English one of his Considered the most suitable version for ordinary people to read Hello, welcome to Yilin Study A good book every day Take you on a dual upgrade journey of thinking and wealth You can guess the title of the book From Nothingness This is a book that studies the Chinese Taoist thought of Wuwei Inaction is an ancient topic There are many studies by domestic scholars Why should we listen to a foreigner talk? What about a topic that Chinese people are tired of talking about? I can bet Even if you are a master of Chinese studies The content of this book will also refresh you Because the author's way of thinking about problems is so special For example, you can think of Sen Gelan who uses brain science Latest research results Have you found an explanation for Zhuangzi’s inaction? This book mainly talks about three levels of meaning: There is also the liberalism pursued by modern people Third Confucius, Laozi, Mencius and Zhuangzi Below we will focus on these three levels of meaning. Let’s look at the first level of meaning first Wuwei means doing nothing What is inaction? Many people say that inaction means doing nothing. Like some people in ancient China Zhuangzi, the Seven Sages of the Bamboo Forest, etc. These people are not after money Not involved in politics Run to hide in the deep mountains and old forests passive avoidance Inaction is a non-positive outlook on life According to Sen Gelan’s point of view This is actually the misunderstanding that most people have about Wuwei. He said that inaction means not doing things. But it's done very naturally Make people feel that he is effortless For example, Zhuangzi tells the story of the cook and the cow When the cook slaughters the cow Skilled in sword skills There is no need to consciously think about where to cut. How to cut It was natural to separate the cows. This is a state of inaction In fact, similar situations often occur in reality For example, NBA stars are in great shape when playing basketball. How can you invest in that kind of thing? The performance of being in a deserted place There are also relaxed improvisational performances by jazz musicians In essence, it is the same as what Zhuangzi said about the cook and the ox. This is inaction so Doing nothing is not a life choice that is indifferent to the world. Or chicken soup for the soul when the years are quiet It is actually a personal state Sen Gelan said that inaction means not pursuing deliberately And do things to the level of inaction It's not like I didn't do anything Instead, it requires hard practice Many etiquette regulations of ancient China In fact, it is all about cultivating people into a state of inaction. For example Ancient Chinese people had to recite many classics from childhood And learn etiquette Know how far to bend when bowing How to enter the room And when you sit, sit up straight The purpose of these basic training Just to make you reach a state of gentleness and gentleness On the one hand, you must have been trained Only educated people would do this On the other hand, you acted very sincerely and naturally This kind of nature is the kind of nature that is like cooking a ox. He and an untrained person It’s natural to go to that point stupidly Completely two different things In our daily lives, we ordinary people Sometimes you can enter a state of inaction When you have the ability and confidence to get along well with others You go to a job interview We will have a very harmonious conversation Speak very sincerely Both sides acted very naturally Maybe the time will pass quickly You don't even notice how you behave The result is the best performance But many people can't do this As soon as I enter the room, I don’t know which leg to take first. Afraid that my handshake posture is wrong I think about every word I say for a long time Obviously this does not mean doing nothing The result will be poor performance. Of course, if Wuwei is explained like this you may think There doesn't seem to be anything too clever about it. Then listen carefully to what I say next You might find it interesting The real brilliance of Sen Gelan is that He learned from modern brain science Found a theoretical explanation for Wuwei Some people have read a best-selling book It’s called thinking fast and slow This book roughly divides human thinking into two systems a fast automatic A system that operates by itself without any subjective effort One It is a slow, deliberate and effortful system 2. If I ask you which one weighs more, a pound of iron or a pound of cotton? The system in your brain reacts very quickly Answered instantly Of course, iron is heavier than cotton. But after a second Your system 2 has just started He analyzed it carefully Although iron is heavier than cotton But it’s all a pound So it should be the same weight This is the basic routine for the two systems to work. Sen Gelan made some developments on this theory He reacts quickly System 1 that operates unconsciously is called thermal cognition slow down reaction System 2, which can only be run with consciousness, is called cold cognition Thermal cognitive systems are largely unconscious For example, some of our daily actions Like walking or driving You don’t need to think about the action points first can be completed automatically But the cold cognitive system needs to make judgments at any time Time consuming and laborious But its advantage is that it is more rational Ability to adjust at any time in the face of new information Set new priorities for different tasks The state of inaction we mentioned earlier In fact, these two cognitive systems made some changes There is a huge gap between us and ordinary people So how did it change? We need to look at a psychological experiment first This experiment is actually very simple I just give you a blank piece of paper There are a few words written on the paper For example, red green green red green Then let you read it again I believe there is no pressure on everyone Read it in one breath Then I will give you another piece of paper These words are still written on the paper But each word is written in a different color For example, the word red But I will write it for you with a green pen What about the word green? I write with a red pen Then I'll let you read it again But this time I don’t read the word literally. Rather read what the actual color of the word is The result is What most people read is stumbling. after reading a word Stop for a while Only then can you think clearly about how to pronounce the next word This experiment is very famous in psychology It has a history of seventy or eighty years called stroop task These words are originally very simple We don’t need to use cold cognition It can be read through the unconscious state of thermal cognition. But after I make the word red appear green The meaning of the word conflicts with its actual color This interferes with your thermal perception But it runs automatically by thermal cognition There is no way to complete the task So Leng Cognition took action and controlled your movements. Your reaction slows down Actually There are two areas in the human brain responsible for cognitive control The first area is called the anterior cingulate cortex It functions like a smoke alarm, right? Just keep monitoring the situation on Once he discovers something unusual, will give the second area called lateral prefrontal cortex alarm And the role of this lateral prefrontal cortex It's like a fire brigade Its more important function is the decision-making system in the brain It gives orders to various parts of the brain handle the situation So when you see a handwriting that is green when the word red The smoke alarm will alert the fire brigade Fire brigade comparison game rules will instruct the visual system to ignore the meaning of the word instead focus on the color of the word And tell your word processing system to shut up Don't pronounce it literally An unskilled person does this Troop Mission These two cognitive control systems need to be continuously activated Wait until you become proficient You can automatically only see the color ignore literal meaning Then the entire task is handed over to thermal cognition Completed in a subconscious state And save your cold cognition to do other things So from this experiment we can see Deliberately That is, the smoke alarm and the fire brigade are turned on at the same time. Tasks mainly consist of slow responses The brain-consuming cold cognitive system is completed The whole movement is conscious And skilled That is, the smoke alarm and the fire brigade are turned off at the same time. The task is mainly completed by the fast-responsive thermal cognitive system The whole action is unconscious So what is the state of inaction? In fact, it is a state one level higher than proficiency. He can turn off smoke alarms and fire brigade one open Sen Gelan introduced a particularly powerful experiment Scientist plays while jazz pianist plays Scan his brain with an MRI this pianist The performance was a piece he was very familiar with. And add some improvisation That is, entering a state of inaction Scan found At this time, the fire brigade in his mind is turned off. But his alarm intensified instead. This means that The pianist does not consciously control his body It’s not about how the fingers move but at the same time He has a very alert perception of his surroundings It seems like this Nothing more than consciousness and subconsciousness The perfect combination of cold cognition and hot cognition Consciousness lets go of the body Let your body do something spontaneously and naturally And at the same time Consciousness remains highly acute This is really a rare state Let me give you another football example and you will understand better. Dragan, the former head coach of Jiangsu Sainty Team, Chinese players have criticized He said that the basic skills of Chinese players are pretty good. The action of making the pass from the bottom is very precise. But consciousness is not good Players often run into that position Just make a standard cross But this player No matter what the situation is in the restricted area now This is just proficiency But it didn’t reach Wuwei Look at the passing moves of those big-name players So skilled too so natural But people still have smoke alarms on in their heads. He can adapt to different situations on the field Adjust the passing route But this adjustment is very natural It's not the kind of thing where you stop and look twice. The deliberate action of the third pass You see, from a cognitive science perspective inaction and proficiency There is only a small difference between inaction and deliberateness This little bit is worth a master’s lifetime pursuit. This is the author’s first meaning about Wuwei Do something particularly relaxing Now let’s look at the second level of meaning We just talked about What exactly is the state of inaction? Maybe some of my friends have studied psychology There will be this question psychologically Talk about the inaction you mentioned and flow in psychology Isn’t it the same thing as flow? Indeed, the two concepts are very similar but The author Sen Gelan spends a lot of space in the book to explain The important difference between the two concepts of inaction and flow Let’s first take a look at what flow is so-called flow It means you are working I entered such an immersive state while doing it You don’t seem to have any need for self-control at this time. can concentrate highly Have extremely high work efficiency And totally attracted by what I'm doing So that I forget myself ignored time Maybe programmers know what I'm talking about They often enter this state But flow is actually not a mystery Not uncommon either I dare say that all people engaged in mental work Have you ever had this kind of selfless experience? But In fact, we have many selfless experiences in life. For example, watching TV series immersed in the drama Chat and play cards with friends Play simple mini-games Play on mobile phone and surf the Internet Do these experiences count as flow? to this end Psychologists specifically added two conditions to the new flow First, the activity you are engaged in Must be a challenge to you second This activity must involve your complex skills Sen Gelan disagrees with these two standards. He said if you investigate The raw data collected by those psychologists Many people report that they enter a flow state then It’s not a complicated job at all. Not really a challenge But also very satisfied and selfless Sen Gelan proposed Distinguish between a good self-forgetting experience and a bad self-forgetting experience Another standard should be used It's after you've done this feel very satisfied still feel empty If the selfless experience makes you feel satisfied Just do nothing So if you look at it this way Inaction is a state higher than flow It not only makes you feel selfless It also makes you feel meaningful That inaction What makes you feel that doing things is meaningful? The author says that this depends on the value system Chinese sages talk about inaction and flow The biggest difference Because inaction conforms to a certain value system It is a spiritual pursuit We often see ancient sages mention two words: One is heaven One is Tao Chinese people don’t talk about God There is no monotheism But starting from the Zhou Dynasty Chinese people are very trusting For example, to this day We also like to say that the sky has eyes and so on In the eyes of our ancestors God has spirit Maybe a personality He nourishes all things And full of goodwill If you do things according to the laws of heaven God will reward you ancient sages The value system given to Wuwei Just do things in line with the Tao more specifically You relax yourself Give yourself to heaven The way of heaven is what you think does exist And something greater than you Wait until you reach the state of inaction God will give you a reward This is virtue In today's terms Virtue seems to be a kind of energy It could also be a kind of radiation Or a kind of halo If a person is virtuous Then the people around him will automatically be attracted to him virtue as the ancients said Morality explained to us today But it's different Virtue is not a quality of a good person But it strengthens a kind of strength The ancients practiced Taoism Not to remind myself to be a good person But to gain the mana of virtue At this point you may be asking That's not right The values that the ancients assigned to Wuwei are heaven, Tao, and morality. But this kind of thing has obvious religious connotations So modern people no longer believe in the religion of heaven. How should we pursue inaction? the answer is You can use any value system To replace the way of heaven Liberalism is a system of values It can also be said to be the religion of modern people You can also add other things to it For example, knowledge, family, friendship, nature, etc. The key is You have to believe that the system is something bigger than yourself If you do this Consistent with your value system Entered into a state of selflessness I've also heard a particularly popular theory What is happiness? Happiness is being a part of something much bigger than you We look at outstanding entrepreneurs Everyone likes to say that a company must have goals, missions and values. That's what it means These spiritual things Gave them a selfless experience And those who pursue inaction Embrace a value system to yourself There is another function That's social Do you think the ancients said that if a person is virtuous? This actually makes sense Because inaction is a particularly natural and spontaneous state There is no way to fake this kind of nature When you are in a state of inaction You are indeed charming People just trust you and get close to you like many politicians No matter how fluently this person memorizes speeches, As soon as we saw him, we felt that he was very fake. Every gesture is full of artificiality Nothing more than a sophisticated egoist Not supported by a value system This person must use willpower Do it deliberately This is a completely different effect So from the perspective of doing things Inaction can help us perform better Socially speaking Inaction can help us get along well with others influence the others Even governing the country This is the second meaning of Wuwei It is to achieve the state of inaction
